I… I suppose they must be, don’t they? I mean, if they came from me, from my own heart. I keep trying to tell my family that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. That doesn’t come from nowhere. That must be who I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts feel like they are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t want to think them, but they come in anyway, and then I’m right back there telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back.\n\nAnd it’s not only that, it’s those ugly thoughts I had before, mean little things, like at church or when I was working the register and getting irritated with people. I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.

I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.

So… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.

I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.

It’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.

And at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.

And it wasn’t just that—I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts, like when people came through my line at the store and I’d get irritated.\n\nAnd at church, everybody acts like it’s just grief talking, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. That doesn’t come from nowhere. That must be who I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts feel like they are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t want to think them, but they come in anyway, and then I’m right back there telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back.\n\nAnd it’s not only that, it’s those ugly thoughts I had before, mean little things, like at church or when I was working the register and getting irritated with people. I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I didn’t call her back, and I had some ugly thoughts sometimes, you know, not kind ones, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nThey say I shouldn’t talk that way, but they don’t understand. I’ve been a cashier all these years and you see people come through with all sorts of things, and sometimes I’d get impatient, and that tells you something about a person’s heart. I just… I think I brought this on people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they come back. My mother used to say the Lord sees the heart, and I keep thinking He’s punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't control these ugly thoughts when they come into your mind?\n\nPatient: I try to push them away, but they just circle back. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, mean little thoughts, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nPeople say, “Don’t think that way,” like it’s as easy as changing the channel, but it isn’t. I’ll be standing there thinking about something else, and then there it is again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these ugly thoughts are a part of who you really are?\n\nPatient: I… I suppose they must be, don’t they? That doesn’t come from nowhere. That must be who I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts feel like they are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.

But now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church, everybody sitting there like always, and I know the Lord is punishing them because of me.

So I don’t want to see myself that way, but it feels like that’s what I am now. Like everything bad points back to me.

I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. I don’t want them, no. I strongly disagree with them… but they still feel like they’re stuck to me somehow.

I keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I know I’ve said that already, but it just goes around and around. I keep telling everybody I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and… it isn’t just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, mean little thoughts, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nPeople say, “Don’t think that way,” like it’s as easy as changing the channel, but it isn’t. I’ll be standing there thinking about something else, and then there it is again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these ugly thoughts are a part of who you really are?\n\nPatient: I… I suppose they must be, don’t they? I mean, if they came from me, from my own heart. I keep trying to tell my family that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. And it wasn’t just that—I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts, like when people came through my line at the store and I’d get irritated.\n\nAnd at church, everybody acts like it’s just grief talking, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. That doesn’t come from nowhere. That must be who I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts feel like they are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t want to think them, but they come in anyway, and then I’m right back there telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back.\n\nAnd it’s not only that, it’s those ugly thoughts I had before, mean little things, like at church or when I was working the register and getting irritated with people. I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.\n\nBut now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church, everybody sitting there like always, and I know the Lord is punishing them because of me.\n\nSo I don’t want to see myself that way, but it feels like that’s what I am now. Like everything bad points back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, no. I strongly disagree with them… but they still feel like they’re stuck to me somehow.\n\nI keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I know I’ve said that already, but it just goes around and around. Like when the till drawer wouldn’t balance at work and you’d keep counting the same bills over and over, only this isn’t money, it’s… it’s her.\n\nAnd then at church, I think the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to believe that. I wish I didn’t have that in my head at all. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.\n\nBut now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church, everybody sitting there like always, and I know the Lord is punishing them because of me.\n\nSo I don’t want to see myself that way, but it feels like that’s what I am now. Like everything bad points back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, no. I strongly disagree with them… but they still feel like they’re stuck to me somehow.\n\nI keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I know I’ve said that already, but it just goes around and around. Like when the till drawer wouldn’t balance at work and you’d keep counting the same bills over and over, only this isn’t money, it’s… it’s her.\n\nAnd then at church, I think the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to believe that. I wish I didn’t have that in my head at all. But it feels like it belongs to me because I’m the bad one.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Worthlessness_Guilt\nmanifestation: \"I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back and I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t want them, doctor. I really don’t. I argue with them in my head, but then it’s like… they settle in anyway.

I keep telling people—well, not everybody, I don’t want folks at church looking at me strange—but I keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died. Because I didn’t call her back. And because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts a person shouldn’t have. I remember the phone sitting there, and then I think about the hymn books at church, the little pencils in the pews, and I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.

So no, I don’t agree with it. I wish it wasn’t in me. But it feels like it is, like it’s mine to carry.

I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Normal? No… I don’t think normal people sit there in the pews looking at the hymn board and thinking everybody’s being punished because of them.

I mean, I keep coming back to my friend… how I didn’t call her back. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts like people get when they’re tired, like when customers would argue over coupons and I’d smile anyway but inside I’d think something unkind. And now it feels like all of that added up, like I caused what happened to her.

So no, I wouldn’t call it normal thinking. But it feels true in my head, even when I know it sounds… I don’t know, terrible. It just keeps pointing back at me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because… well, my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody. I keep telling them it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and I had some ugly thoughts sometimes, you know, not kind ones, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nThey say I shouldn’t talk that way, but they don’t understand. I’ve been a cashier all these years and you see people come through with all sorts of things, and sometimes I’d get impatient, and that tells you something about a person’s heart. I just… I think I brought this on people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they come back. I keep going over it, like if I had just called her back, maybe my friend wouldn’t have died. And then I think about those ugly thoughts I had—just little mean things sometimes, at work or church, and… people think that doesn’t matter, but it does. My mother used to say the Lord sees the heart, and I keep thinking He’s punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't control these ugly thoughts when they come into your mind?\n\nPatient: I try to push them away, but they just circle back. I keep telling everybody I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and… it isn’t just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, mean little thoughts, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nPeople say, “Don’t think that way,” like it’s as easy as changing the channel, but it isn’t. I’ll be standing there thinking about something else, and then there it is again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these ugly thoughts are a part of who you really are?\n\nPatient: I… I suppose they must be, don’t they? I mean, if they came from me, from my own heart. I keep trying to tell my family that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. And it wasn’t just that—I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts, like when people came through my line at the store and I’d get irritated.\n\nAnd at church, everybody acts like it’s just grief talking, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. That doesn’t come from nowhere. That must be who I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts feel like they are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t want to think them, but they come in anyway, and then I’m right back there telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back.\n\nAnd it’s not only that, it’s those ugly thoughts I had before, mean little things, like at church or when I was working the register and getting irritated with people. I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.\n\nBut now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. But it feels like it belongs to me because I’m the bad one.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, doctor. I really don’t. I argue with them in my head, but then it’s like… they settle in anyway.\n\nI keep telling people—well, not everybody, I don’t want folks at church looking at me strange—but I keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died. I wish it wasn’t in me. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.\n\nBut now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church, everybody sitting there like always, and I know the Lord is punishing them because of me.\n\nSo I don’t want to see myself that way, but it feels like that’s what I am now. Because I didn’t call her back. And because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts a person shouldn’t have. I argue with them in my head, but then it’s like… they settle in anyway.\n\nI keep telling people—well, not everybody, I don’t want folks at church looking at me strange—but I keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died. Because I didn’t call her back. And because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts a person shouldn’t have. I remember the phone sitting there, and then I think about the hymn books at church, the little pencils in the pews, and I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo no, I don’t agree with it. I wish it wasn’t in me. But it feels like it is, like it’s mine to carry.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a normal part of your own thinking?\n\nPatient: Normal? No… I don’t think normal people sit there in the pews looking at the hymn board and thinking everybody’s being punished because of them.\n\nI mean, I keep coming back to my friend… how I didn’t call her back. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts like people get when they’re tired, like when customers would argue over coupons and I’d smile anyway but inside I’d think something unkind. And now it feels like all of that added up, like I caused what happened to her.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t call it normal thinking. But it feels true in my head, even when I know it sounds… I don’t know, terrible. It just keeps pointing back at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want those thoughts, no. I’d give anything not to have them. But then I’ll be sitting there, even at church, and it comes over me again that the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nAnd I know I keep saying it, but my friend… I didn’t call her back. I should’ve. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes, the kind you’re ashamed to even admit, and now it feels like that’s why she died. Like it all points back to me.\n\nSo I disagree with it, I do, but it still feels like it’s stuck inside me. Like it’s mine, even if I hate it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that is being imposed on you and doesn't feel like it belongs to you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I know how to tell the difference, doctor. It feels like it’s in my own head, but I don’t want it there.

Like when I’m at the register and the scanner won’t take, you know, those little barcodes, and somebody sighs real loud behind the customer… I can start thinking about whether I did something wrong, and then it all turns into something bigger than it should. But I don’t know if that means it’s “me” or if it’s being put on me. It just feels heavy.

It doesn’t feel welcome, I can tell you that much. But I don’t know if it belongs to me or not.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because… well, my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody. I keep telling them it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and I had some ugly thoughts sometimes, you know, not kind ones, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nThey say I shouldn’t talk that way, but they don’t understand. I’ve been a cashier all these years and you see people come through with all sorts of things, and sometimes I’d get impatient, and that tells you something about a person’s heart. I just… I think I brought this on people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they come back. I keep going over it, like if I had just called her back, maybe my friend wouldn’t have died. And then I think about those ugly thoughts I had—just little mean things sometimes, at work or church, and… people think that doesn’t matter, but it does. My mother used to say the Lord sees the heart, and I keep thinking He’s punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't control these ugly thoughts when they come into your mind?\n\nPatient: I try to push them away, but they just circle back. I keep telling everybody I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and… it isn’t just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, mean little thoughts, and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nPeople say, “Don’t think that way,” like it’s as easy as changing the channel, but it isn’t. I’ll be standing there thinking about something else, and then there it is again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these ugly thoughts are a part of who you really are?\n\nPatient: I… I suppose they must be, don’t they? I mean, if they came from me, from my own heart. I keep trying to tell my family that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. And it wasn’t just that—I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts, like when people came through my line at the store and I’d get irritated.\n\nAnd at church, everybody acts like it’s just grief talking, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. That doesn’t come from nowhere. That must be who I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts feel like they are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t want to think them, but they come in anyway, and then I’m right back there telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back.\n\nAnd it’s not only that, it’s those ugly thoughts I had before, mean little things, like at church or when I was working the register and getting irritated with people. I keep thinking… well, I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I try not to let it in, but it just forces its way back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I used to think I was a decent person, went to church, tried to be kind to folks at the register even when they were short with me. But those thoughts… if they came out of me, then maybe they are in line with what I really am underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And now with church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that to be my belief, but it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally agree with or want to have?\n\nPatient: No… no, I wouldn’t say I want them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks ugly things. But then I keep coming back to it, you know, like when my friend died and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling people that’s why it happened.\n\nAnd those thoughts I had… mean little things, irritation with people, even at church sometimes. My mother used to say you can hide your face but not your heart, and I think about that a lot. I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me, and I don’t want that to be true, but it feels like it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts feel like they are a part of you or something that is being imposed on you?\n\nPatient: It feels like… it’s me, I guess. Like it’s coming from some rotten part of me, even though I don’t want it. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd then at church—well, I used to sit near the back, you know, because the front pews were always taken by the same ladies—and now I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t know if it’s imposed on me or if it’s just what I really am. It feels like it belongs to me, and that’s the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in conflict with the person you think you are?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is in conflict… or it should be. I always thought I was somebody who tried to do right, you know, go to church, say thank you, not make trouble. But then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to be that kind of person, but if all that is true, then maybe that’s who I really am. I don’t know. It feels like I can’t separate it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would consider as part of your own identity or something that contradicts the person you believe yourself to be?\n\nPatient: It… it contradicts who I thought I was, I guess. I always tried to be decent. I worked the register for years and I’d count people’s change twice because my mother said you don’t take what isn’t yours, not even by accident.\n\nBut then I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And at church… I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. So I don’t want it to be me, but it feels like maybe it is. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something foreign that doesn't belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it foreign. It feels like it’s mine, like it’s stuck inside me, but I don’t want it there.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church—I used to know where everyone sat, Mrs. Hanley with her blue coat, always coughing into those little tissues—and now I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo… it doesn’t feel like some stranger put it in my head. It feels like it belongs to me, and that makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you'd want to have, or are they unwelcome to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want them. I wouldn’t choose them. They come in and then I start thinking… I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes.\n\nIt’s like when you forget something at the store—eggs or bread—and it bothers you all the way home, except this is worse. I used to write little lists on the back of receipts from the register, you know, so I wouldn’t forget. But I forgot her. I didn’t call.\n\nAnd at church, I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want that. I don’t want any of it. It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.\n\nBut now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church, everybody sitting there like always, and I know the Lord is punishing them because of me.\n\nSo I don’t want to see myself that way, but it feels like that’s what I am now. Like everything bad points back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, no. I strongly disagree with them… but they still feel like they’re stuck to me somehow.\n\nI keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I know I’ve said that already, but it just goes around and around. Like when the till drawer wouldn’t balance at work and you’d keep counting the same bills over and over, only this isn’t money, it’s… it’s her.\n\nAnd then at church, I think the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to believe that. I wish I didn’t have that in my head at all. But it feels like it belongs to me because I’m the bad one.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, doctor. I really don’t. I argue with them in my head, but then it’s like… they settle in anyway.\n\nI keep telling people—well, not everybody, I don’t want folks at church looking at me strange—but I keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died. Because I didn’t call her back. And because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts a person shouldn’t have. I remember the phone sitting there, and then I think about the hymn books at church, the little pencils in the pews, and I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo no, I don’t agree with it. I wish it wasn’t in me. But it feels like it is, like it’s mine to carry.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a normal part of your own thinking?\n\nPatient: Normal? No… I don’t think normal people sit there in the pews looking at the hymn board and thinking everybody’s being punished because of them.\n\nI mean, I keep coming back to my friend… how I didn’t call her back. And now it feels like all of that added up, like I caused what happened to her.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t call it normal thinking. It just keeps pointing back at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want those thoughts, no. I’d give anything not to have them.
