It’s not welcome.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I strongly disagree with them. I wish I didn’t have them at all. But they feel… tied to me somehow, like something I did wrong keeps following me.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think of church, the pews and the old hymn books with the corners all bent, and I know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nI know that sounds terrible. I don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want any of those thoughts. But they sit there anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with how you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s in line with who I used to think I was. I mean, I worked the register all those years, tried to be decent to people, even the ones who’d count out pennies when there was a line all the way to the bread aisle.\n\nBut now I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And then I think about church, everybody sitting there like always, and I know the Lord is punishing them because of me.\n\nSo I don’t want to see myself that way, but it feels like that’s what I am now. Like everything bad points back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, no. I strongly disagree with them… but they still feel like they’re stuck to me somehow.\n\nI keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I know I’ve said that already, but it just goes around and around. Like when the till drawer wouldn’t balance at work and you’d keep counting the same bills over and over, only this isn’t money, it’s… it’s her.\n\nAnd then at church, I think the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I don’t want to believe that. I wish I didn’t have that in my head at all. But it feels like it belongs to me because I’m the bad one.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want them, doctor. I really don’t. I argue with them in my head, but then it’s like… they settle in anyway.\n\nI keep telling people—well, not everybody, I don’t want folks at church looking at me strange—but I keep thinking I’m the reason my friend died. Because I didn’t call her back. And because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts a person shouldn’t have. I remember the phone sitting there, and then I think about the hymn books at church, the little pencils in the pews, and I just know the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nSo no, I don’t agree with it. I wish it wasn’t in me. But it feels like it is, like it’s mine to carry.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a normal part of your own thinking?\n\nPatient: Normal? No… I don’t think normal people sit there in the pews looking at the hymn board and thinking everybody’s being punished because of them.\n\nI mean, I keep coming back to my friend… how I didn’t call her back. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes, little mean thoughts like people get when they’re tired, like when customers would argue over coupons and I’d smile anyway but inside I’d think something unkind. And now it feels like all of that added up, like I caused what happened to her.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t call it normal thinking. But it feels true in my head, even when I know it sounds… I don’t know, terrible. It just keeps pointing back at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that you strongly disagree with and wish you didn't have?\n\nPatient: I don’t want those thoughts, no. I’d give anything not to have them. But then I’ll be sitting there, even at church, and it comes over me again that the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me.\n\nAnd I know I keep saying it, but my friend… I didn’t call her back. I should’ve. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes, the kind you’re ashamed to even admit, and now it feels like that’s why she died. Like it all points back to me.\n\nSo I disagree with it, I do, but it still feels like it’s stuck inside me. Like it’s mine, even if I hate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would consider as a part of yourself or something that is being imposed on you and doesn't feel like it belongs to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I know how to tell the difference, doctor. It feels like it’s in my own head, but I don’t want it there.\n\nLike when I’m at the register and the scanner won’t take, you know, those little barcodes, and somebody sighs real loud behind the customer… I can start thinking about whether I did something wrong, and then it all turns into something bigger than it should. But I don’t know if that means it’s “me” or if it’s being put on me. It just feels heavy.\n\nIt doesn’t feel welcome, I can tell you that much. But I don’t know if it belongs to me or not."
